# BuilderGate Stability

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Document Type | scope_srs |
| Scope | BGSTAB |
| Scope Name | BuilderGate Stability |

## 1. Scope Overview

This scope specifies bounded runtime resource limits, browser input backpressure, visible terminal output scheduling, snapshot storage budgets, terminal runtime residency, and split WebSocket transport behavior for BuilderGate terminal stability.

## 2. Scope Boundaries

### In Scope

- Runtime-configured caps for WebSocket, terminal output/input, snapshot storage, and terminal runtime residency.
- Browser WebSocket send-buffer backpressure for user input.
- Visible terminal output scheduling and authoritative recovery after overflow.
- Snapshot localStorage budget and tombstone TTL handling.
- Hidden terminal runtime LRU residency without deleting persistent workspace/tab state.
- Feature-flagged split WebSocket handshake, routing, fallback, and rollback-safe behavior.
- Negotiated binary wire format for the terminal output, checkpoint, replay, and screen-repair data plane, including its default flip and reversible rollback.
- Non-2002 validation requirements for agent-run live checks.

### Out of Scope

- Making split WebSocket the default socket topology. This exclusion covers socket topology only and does not restrict the terminal data plane wire format, which is in scope.
- Replacing xterm.js or the terminal rendering engine.
- Changing AI TUI idle/running semantic invariants.
- Using `https://localhost:2002` for agent-run validation.
- Terminating all `node.exe` processes during validation.

## 3. Assumptions and Constraints

- The default WebSocket transport mode remains `unified`.
- The terminal data plane wire format is controlled by `realtime.terminalWireFormat`, which is independent of `wsTransportMode`; the control plane stays JSON in every mode.
- Split WebSocket is a feature-flagged transport mode and must remain rollback-safe.
- Browser main-thread rendering stalls are mitigated by visible output scheduling; split WebSocket alone is not sufficient.
- Agent-run live validation must use `https://localhost:2222`; health checks use `http://localhost:2221/health`.
- Raw terminal input must not be written to debug logs when backpressure or reconnect handling occurs.

## 4. Requirements

### FR-BGSTAB-001 — Runtime resource limits for terminal stability

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 0.5.5-buildergate-stability |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | buildergate, stability, runtime-config, feasibility:high |

| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/2026-06-17.buildergate-remaining-stability-work-implementation-plan.md, docs/research/2026-06-17.buildergate-remaining-stability-work-implementation-completion-report.md |

#### Requirement

The system shall expose and consume runtime resource limits for terminal input, terminal output, snapshot storage, workspace runtime residency, and WebSocket transport so stability limits are configured centrally instead of through scattered hard-coded constants.

#### Rationale

The implementation plan identifies unbounded or scattered queue/cache limits as a root cause of memory growth and input delay. Central runtime limits are the foundation for every later stability cap.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Given the public runtime config includes client WebSocket, terminal, snapshot, workspace runtime, and realtime transport settings, when the frontend initializes, then typed getters expose validated values with server-compatible defaults.
- [ ] AC-2: Given a runtime config payload contains invalid, negative, non-finite, or inconsistent limit values, when the frontend parses the payload, then it falls back to safe defaults through a test-observable path.
- [ ] AC-3: Given the runtime config publishes wsTransportMode, when it is unified, split-shadow, or split, then frontend and server parsing preserve that exact supported mode; unsupported modes fall back to unified.

### FR-BGSTAB-002 — Browser WebSocket input backpressure

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 0.5.5-buildergate-stability |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | buildergate, stability, websocket, input, feasibility:high |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | stable |
| Verification Method | WebSocket backpressure unit tests and focused frontend stability tests. |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/2026-06-17.buildergate-remaining-stability-work-implementation-plan.md |

#### Requirement

The browser client shall gate terminal input WebSocket messages by observing WebSocket.bufferedAmount, payload byte size, and runtime client WebSocket limits. Control and recovery messages shall continue to bypass input backpressure to avoid replay or repair deadlocks.

#### Rationale

Keyboard input delay can grow when terminal input keeps entering an already backed-up browser send queue. Input-only gating limits memory growth without blocking snapshot, repair, or subscription recovery traffic.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Given a terminal input payload is below configured WebSocket thresholds, when send() is called, then the payload is sent normally.
- [ ] AC-2: Given bufferedAmount or bufferedAmount plus payloadBytes exceeds the soft input threshold, when an input message is sent, then the send result is client-backpressure and the input enters a bounded retry path.
- [ ] AC-3: Given bufferedAmount exceeds the hard reconnect threshold, when an input message is sent, then the send result is client-hard-backpressure and a reconnect path is requested.
- [ ] AC-4: Given a control, subscribe, screen snapshot ACK, or screen repair ACK message is sent while the browser send buffer is high, then that control message is not blocked by input backpressure.
- [ ] AC-5: Given the retry path observes bufferedAmount below the low-water threshold and the queued input is sent, then the input barrier is cleared through the normal transport state path.
- [ ] AC-6: Given a backpressured input is queued or rejected, then raw input content is not written to debug output.

#### Verification Evidence

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | frontend/tests/unit/webSocketBackpressure.test.ts | AC-1..AC-4 | Focused browser backpressure tests passed in current verification. |
| VE-2 | test | frontend/tests/unit/runtimeConfig.test.ts | AC-2..AC-3 | Runtime client WebSocket limits covered. |

### FR-BGSTAB-003 — Visible terminal output scheduling and recovery

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 0.5.5-buildergate-stability |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | buildergate, stability, xterm, output, feasibility:high |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | stable |
| Verification Method | Terminal output scheduler unit tests and visible output recovery unit tests. |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/2026-06-17.buildergate-remaining-stability-work-implementation-plan.md |

#### Requirement

Visible terminal output shall be written through a bounded scheduler that applies chunk and byte limits, preserves callback order, avoids splitting code points, and requests authoritative recovery when pending local output exceeds configured limits.

#### Rationale

Writing unbounded live output directly to xterm can monopolize the browser main thread and make keyboard input feel progressively slower. The scheduler bounds local work and shifts recovery to server-authoritative snapshot or repair paths.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Given visible terminal output is enqueued under the configured budget, when the scheduler drains, then it writes output in bounded chunks and preserves callback order.
- [ ] AC-2: Given output contains multibyte characters, when the scheduler splits work, then it does not split code points.
- [ ] AC-3: Given queued visible output exceeds configured chunk or byte limits, when enqueue runs, then not-yet-written local output is bounded and an overflow or recovery result is observable.
- [ ] AC-4: Given visible overflow marks a terminal stale, when authoritative snapshot or repair recovery completes, then the stale output state can be cleared without retaining an unbounded local queue.
- [ ] AC-5: Given the scheduler is reset, then stale callbacks from canceled writes do not mutate the new terminal state.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VE-1 | test | frontend/tests/unit/terminalOutputScheduler.test.ts | AC-1..AC-3, AC-5 | Focused scheduler tests passed in current verification. |
| VE-2 | test | frontend/tests/unit/visibleOutputRecovery.test.ts | AC-4 | Focused recovery tests passed in current verification. |

#### Trace Links

- Treat xterm write callbacks as parse completion, not proof of paint completion. Do not mix authoritative snapshot or repair replacement with live output queue contents.

### FR-BGSTAB-004 — Terminal snapshot storage budgets and tombstone expiry

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 0.5.5-buildergate-stability |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | buildergate, stability, snapshots, localstorage, feasibility:high |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | stable |
| Verification Method | Terminal snapshot unit tests. |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/2026-06-17.buildergate-remaining-stability-work-implementation-plan.md |

#### Requirement

Terminal snapshot persistence shall enforce runtime-configured per-snapshot size, total storage budget, maximum entry count, and removal tombstone TTL so localStorage does not grow or remain blocked indefinitely during long-running sessions.

#### Rationale

Runtime eviction and terminal remount increase snapshot usage. Snapshot storage must therefore be bounded before runtime residency eviction can safely increase restore frequency.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Given a snapshot exceeds the per-snapshot character limit, when save or restore parsing runs, then the oversized snapshot is rejected without expanding storage.
- [ ] AC-2: Given total snapshot storage exceeds the configured budget, when quota recovery or eviction runs, then older or corrupt entries are evicted until the budget is satisfied.
- [ ] AC-3: Given the number of snapshot entries exceeds maxEntries, when eviction runs, then older eligible entries are removed even if total character budget is not exceeded.
- [ ] AC-4: Given a removal tombstone is fresh, when a save for that session is attempted, then the tombstone blocks save for that current removal intent.
- [ ] AC-5: Given a removal tombstone is expired, legacy, malformed, or missing a valid timestamp, when snapshot storage is checked, then it does not permanently block future snapshots.

#### Verification Evidence

| VE-1 | test | frontend/tests/unit/terminalSnapshot.test.ts | AC-1..AC-5 | Focused snapshot tests passed in current verification. |

### FR-BGSTAB-005 — Terminal runtime residency limits

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 0.5.5-buildergate-stability |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | buildergate, stability, runtime-residency, lru, feasibility:high |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | stable |
| Verification Method | Runtime residency unit tests. |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/2026-06-17.buildergate-remaining-stability-work-implementation-plan.md |

#### Requirement

BuilderGate shall limit mounted live terminal runtimes with an LRU residency policy that always pins visible terminals, excludes disconnected tabs, respects hidden runtime TTL, and applies configured maximum live workspace and terminal counts without deleting persistent workspace or tab state.

#### Rationale

Long-running sessions can accumulate xterm instances and terminal runtime objects even after the user stops viewing them. Residency limits reduce memory growth while preserving the user's saved workspaces and tabs.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Given a terminal tab is visible in the active view or desktop grid, when residency is computed, then that terminal remains resident even if caps are exceeded.
- [ ] AC-2: Given hidden terminals exceed maxLiveTerminals, when residency is computed, then the oldest eligible hidden terminals are excluded from the runtime layer.
- [ ] AC-3: Given hidden workspaces exceed maxLiveWorkspaces, when residency is computed, then hidden workspace runtimes are excluded while the active workspace remains resident.
- [ ] AC-4: Given a hidden terminal has not exceeded hiddenRuntimeTtlMs, when residency is computed, then it remains resident until the TTL expires.
- [ ] AC-5: Given a tab is disconnected, removed, or restarted, when residency metadata is updated, then stale runtime metadata does not keep that tab resident.

### FR-BGSTAB-006 — Split WebSocket handshake and channel isolation

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 0.5.5-buildergate-stability |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | buildergate, stability, websocket, split-shadow, feasibility:high |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | stable |
| Verification Method | Server split handshake tests, websocket transport parser tests, frontend websocket URL tests, and frontend split lifecycle tests. |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/2026-06-17.buildergate-remaining-stability-work-implementation-plan.md |

#### Requirement

The WebSocket transport shall support unified, split-shadow, and split modes. Split control connections shall receive logical group metadata and a short-lived pair token, while output connections shall be accepted only when the pair token and authenticated identity match the live control group.

#### Rationale

Splitting control/input from output/replay can reduce the chance that output backlog blocks input/control traffic, but the rollout must be feature-flagged, authenticated, and rollback-safe.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Given transport mode is unified, when a legacy WebSocket connects, then the connection stays compatible with the existing single-socket protocol.
- [ ] AC-2: Given transport mode permits split and a control socket connects, then the server returns clientGroupId, connectionId, channel metadata, and a pair token for the output socket.
- [ ] AC-3: Given an output socket presents a wrong pair token, wrong authenticated identity, missing pair fields, or an expired token, then the server rejects the output connection.
- [ ] AC-4: Given a duplicate output socket connects for the same group, then the previous output socket is closed and removed before the new output socket is used.
- [ ] AC-5: Given mode is split-shadow, then output socket connection lifecycle is exercised without moving terminal payload traffic away from the control path.

### FR-BGSTAB-007 — Split WebSocket terminal payload routing and failure recovery

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 0.5.5-buildergate-stability |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | buildergate, stability, websocket, output-recovery, feasibility:high |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | stable |
| Verification Method | Server split routing and fallback tests plus frontend split lifecycle tests. |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/2026-06-17.buildergate-remaining-stability-work-implementation-plan.md, docs/research/2026-06-17.buildergate-remaining-stability-work-implementation-completion-report.md |

#### Requirement

In full split mode, terminal output, replay snapshot payloads, and screen repair payloads shall route through the paired output socket when it is open. Input, resize, subscribe, unsubscribe, ACK, ping, and repair request messages shall remain on the control socket. If the output socket is missing, closed, not open, over hard backpressure, over queue limit, or fails send, terminal payloads shall fall back to the control socket without dropping current, queued, replay-pending, or screen-repair-pending terminal output.

#### Rationale

Splitting sockets only helps input latency if the output lane can fail safely. Output failure must preserve terminal continuity and must not create a new memory leak or replay deadlock.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Given full split mode with a paired output socket, when terminal output, replay snapshot, or screen repair payload is sent, then the payload is sent through the output socket.
- [ ] AC-2: Given full split mode, when input, resize, subscribe, unsubscribe, replay ACK, repair ACK, repair failed, repair request, or ping is sent, then it stays on the control socket.
- [ ] AC-3: Given the output socket is absent or closes, when a terminal payload is routed, then the control socket is used as fallback.
- [ ] AC-4: Given output socket queue overflow, hard limit, not-open, or send-error occurs, then queued and current terminal payloads are rerouted to the control socket without unbounded accumulation.
- [ ] AC-5: Given an output socket closes before a replay snapshot ACK, then full split mode sends a fresh recovery snapshot on control and preserves pending output behind fallback or degraded recovery snapshots.
- [ ] AC-6: Given output failure occurs while screen repair output is pending, then fallback or degraded recovery snapshot ACK flushes that pending output on control.
- [ ] AC-7: Given split-shadow mode and the output socket closes, then control replay recovery is not forced.

#### Verification Evidence

| Evidence ID | Type | Reference | Covers | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

| VE-2 | test | frontend/tests/unit/splitWebSocketLifecycle.test.ts | AC-3 | Frontend output close fallback tests passed in current verification. |

- -

#### Implementation Notes

- recoverSplitOutputFailureOnControl must remain gated to full split; split-shadow must keep the control path as baseline behavior. Fallback or degraded recovery snapshots cannot be assumed to cover rerouted output, so rerouted and repair-pending output must be preserved until ACK flush.
- [2026-08-18] AC-3/AC-4 의 control socket fallback 은 바이너리 terminal payload 에 대해 프레임 형식을 보존한 채 수행된다(IR-BGSTAB-001 AC-6). fallback 시 JSON 재인코딩 경로를 만들지 않는다. 다만 바이너리 인코딩은 wsTransportMode 가 unified 일 때만 활성화되므로(IR-BGSTAB-001 AC-7), split 데이터 평면 채택이 별도로 결정되기 전까지 이 조항은 조건부로 성립한다.

### REL-BGSTAB-001 — Stability validation and non-2002 operational verification

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | reliability |
| Target | 0.5.5-buildergate-stability |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | medium |
| Tags | buildergate, stability, validation, soak, feasibility:medium |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | stable |
| Verification Method | Automated unit/build/server checks plus future non-2002 live and soak validation. |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/2026-06-17.buildergate-remaining-stability-work-implementation-completion-report.md, docs/research/2026-06-18.buildergate-stability-kiwi-live-completion-report.md |

#### Requirement

Automated stability checks shall cover the bounded resource, browser backpressure, visible output scheduler, snapshot budget, runtime residency, and split WebSocket failure recovery paths. Agent-run live validation shall avoid reserved port 2002, shall not terminate all node.exe processes, and shall record any remaining long-running browser memory/input-lag soak gaps.

#### Rationale

The implementation is covered by unit/server/build checks, but long-duration browser memory and live split transport behavior remain operational validation items.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Given the stability implementation changes, when server tests run, then the full server suite and focused split transport tests pass.
- [ ] AC-2: Given the frontend stability utilities and hooks, when focused frontend unit tests run, then runtime config, WebSocket URL/lifecycle, browser backpressure, terminal scheduler, snapshot, residency, and visible recovery tests pass.
- [ ] AC-3: Given frontend production build is run, then typecheck/build passes with no new blocking error; existing chunk-size warnings may remain observable.
- [ ] AC-4: Given an agent-run live BuilderGate validation is needed, then it uses an explicit non-2002 port and stops only the test server process tree it started.
- [ ] AC-5: Given live split mode or long-running soak validation has not been performed, then the completion report records that as remaining operational validation instead of claiming it as complete.

### FR-BGSTAB-008 — Wave 0 baseline config contract recovery

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 0.5.5-buildergate-stability |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | buildergate, stability, wave0, resourceLimits, settings, runtime-config, feasibility:high |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | stable |
| Verification Method | Focused server config/schema/template/settings tests, frontend runtime config unit tests, server build, and frontend typecheck. |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/plug-leak/wave0.md, docs/research/plug-leak/00.index.md |

#### Requirement

The system shall recover the Wave 0 baseline configuration contract by defining typed resource limits, stability mode switches, strict config validation, Settings metadata, persistence support, and a browser-safe runtime configuration projection without changing cleanup, queue, WebSocket send, or frontend runtime behavior defaults.

#### Rationale

Wave 0 is the baseline contract required before later process cleanup, bounded queues, WebSocket backpressure, and frontend residency waves can be implemented safely and rolled back by configuration.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Given a legacy config omits resourceLimits and stabilityModes, when the server config schema parses it, then typed defaults are applied for headless, server WebSocket, browser WebSocket, terminal, snapshots, workspace runtime, telemetry, and stability mode sections.
- [x] AC-2: Given resourceLimits or stabilityModes contain unknown keys, explicit null sections, non-integer or out-of-range values, unsupported enum values, or inconsistent threshold relationships, when the server config schema validates them, then validation fails observably instead of silently stripping or coercing the invalid data.
- [x] AC-3: Given the bootstrap config template and config.json5 example are rendered, then both document the Wave 0 resourceLimits and stabilityModes defaults used by the schema.
- [x] AC-4: Given Settings APIs read or persist editable runtime values, when selected resource limit or stability mode fields are included in the snapshot or patch, then FieldCapability constraints, apply scopes, dry-run persistence, rollback, and apply summaries remain truthful.
- [x] AC-5: Given a browser requests /api/runtime-config, then the response exposes only non-secret public values: inputReliabilityMode, wsTransportMode, browser-needed resourceLimits sections, and frontendRuntimeResidency mode.
- [x] AC-6: Given Wave 0 is complete, then no process cleanup enforcement, headless queue behavior, server WebSocket send policy, or frontend runtime residency behavior is enabled beyond legacy/observe/direct default switches.

### FR-BGSTAB-009 — Wave 1 server session process observation

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 0.5.5-buildergate-stability |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | buildergate, stability, wave1, session-cleanup, process-observation, telemetry, feasibility:high |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | stable |
| Verification Method | Focused server config/schema/template and SessionManager telemetry tests plus full server test suite. |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/plug-leak/wave1.md, docs/research/plug-leak/00.index.md |

#### Requirement

The system shall add observe-only server session process cleanup telemetry that captures session process ownership metadata and cleanup dry-run evidence for session delete, PTY exit, tab delete, workspace delete, and tab restart paths while preserving the existing PTY kill behavior and forbidding process-name or broad kill enforcement in Wave 1.

#### Rationale

Wave 1 must prove which session-owned root process and descendants exist before and after session close before Wave 2 can safely enforce verified process-tree termination. The current static path deletes server session state after pty.kill without proving OS descendant cleanup.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Given a legacy config omits session.processCleanup, when the server config schema parses it, then observe-mode defaults are applied with gracefulWaitMs=750, forceWaitMs=1500, and descendantSampleLimit=64, and invalid mode, null section, non-integer, or out-of-range values are rejected observably.
- [x] AC-2: Given a new server session is created, when the PTY is spawned, then the session stores process metadata including rootPid when available, shell command, shell args, shell type, cwd, platform, backend when known, launchedAt, and best-effort OS start identity without recording raw terminal input or output.
- [x] AC-3: Given a session is deleted directly or through tab delete, workspace delete, or tab restart, when processCleanup.mode is observe, then the existing data.pty.kill() path remains in use and cleanup telemetry records what would be inspected or terminated without adding force-kill behavior beyond the current PTY kill.
- [x] AC-4: Given a PTY exits naturally, when the onExit handler runs, then cleanup observation evidence is recorded with reason process-exit without double-counting a prior explicit delete result for the same session.
- [x] AC-5: Given descendant sampling or PID identity verification is unavailable, mismatched, or inconclusive, when cleanup observation is recorded, then the status is degraded or skipped-unverified and no broad process-name kill, killall, taskkill /IM, shell-constructed command, or unverified descendant kill is attempted.
- [x] AC-6: Given /api/sessions/telemetry is requested by an authenticated caller, then the response includes bounded session cleanup telemetry with mode, attempted/completed/degraded/unverifiedSkipped counters and recent redacted results containing sessionId, reason, rootPid or null, remainingDescendants, cleanupStatus, and recordedAt.

### FR-BGSTAB-010 — Wave 3 session lifecycle semantics and orphan recovery

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 0.5.5-buildergate-stability |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | buildergate, stability, wave3, session-lifecycle, orphan-recovery, feasibility:high |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | stable |
| Verification Method | Server lifecycle/orphan regression tests, route-level direct delete test, frontend typecheck, full server test suite, SpecKiwi strict validation, and independent review-fix-loop. |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/plug-leak/wave3.md, docs/research/plug-leak/00.index.md |

#### Requirement

The system shall apply a shared server session lifecycle model so PTY natural exit, direct session delete, tab delete, tab restart, workspace delete, and server-start orphan recovery consistently finalize session runtime state and persist workspace tab lifecycle metadata without resurrecting intentionally stopped tabs.

#### Rationale

Wave 3 closes the gap left by observe-only Wave 1: PTY natural exit currently records cleanup observation without running the same session finalizer as explicit deletion, and orphan recovery recreates any missing session without distinguishing crash recovery from intentional stop/delete semantics.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Given a PTY exits naturally, when the onExit handler runs, then the same finalization path used by explicit deletion clears timers, detectors, headless state, CWD watcher/temp file state, WebSocket replay/debug state, and removes the session exactly once while broadcasting session:exited with the exit code.
- [x] AC-2: Given session finalization is triggered more than once for the same session by process-exit or explicit delete races, when the later trigger runs, then cleanup observation and runtime disposal remain idempotent and do not double-count or throw.
- [x] AC-3: Given DELETE /api/sessions/:id deletes a session that belongs to a workspace tab, when the route succeeds, then the workspace tab is persisted as stopped and non-recoverable with lifecycleReason direct-session-delete so orphan recovery does not recreate it.
- [x] AC-4: Given checkOrphanTabs() sees a tab whose session is missing, when the tab lifecycle is stopped or recoverable is false, then no replacement session is created and the tab remains skipped from orphan recovery.
- [x] AC-5: Given restartTab creates a replacement session, when creation succeeds, then the tab persists the new session id and incremented generation before the old session is finalized with reason tab-restart; when creation fails, the old session id remains active and is not finalized.
- [x] AC-6: Given workspace delete removes a workspace, when its sessions are finalized, then workspace-owned tab lifecycle is treated as non-recoverable for the delete operation before tabs are removed from persisted state.
- [x] AC-7: Given workspace tab lifecycle metadata is present or absent in persisted workspace JSON, when backend and frontend types load it, then the optional fields remain backward-compatible and no UI visual redesign is required.

### FR-BGSTAB-012 — Wave 4 bounded output queues and WebSocket backpressure

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 0.5.5-buildergate-stability |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | buildergate, stability, wave4, headless-output, websocket, backpressure, feasibility:high |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | stable |
| Verification Method | Server bounded queue tests, headless output ordering tests, WebSocket safe-send/backpressure tests, replay/screen-repair regression tests, full server test suite, SpecKiwi strict validation, and independent sub-agent review. |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/plug-leak/wave4.md, docs/research/plug-leak/00.index.md, docs/research/2026-06-17.buildergate-backend-backpressure-cleanup-plan.md, docs/research/2026-06-24.buildergate-slowdown-session-process-leak-followup.md |

#### Requirement

The system shall bound server output memory growth by replacing unbounded headless pending output arrays and direct WebSocket send paths with byte-capped output queues, a headless drain pump, and a per-client safe-send policy that applies backpressure without killing sessions.

#### Rationale

Wave 4 closes the slowdown path that remains after the process cleanup waves. Long-running terminal output can currently accumulate in unbounded headless pending arrays and direct WebSocket sends only check readyState, so slow headless writes or slow clients can grow server memory and CPU work over time.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Given Wave0 resourceLimits and stabilityModes are loaded, when SessionManager and WsRouter are constructed or reconfigured, then they consume headless and server WebSocket limits while preserving default `headlessQueueMode=observe` and `wsSendMode=direct`, applying headless queue mode and limits to newly created sessions and WebSocket send mode/limits immediately.
- [x] AC-2: Given headless writes are delayed, when output is queued, then the pending queue enforces UTF-8 byte caps and chunk caps with O(1) dequeue behavior and records current/max pending bytes, chunk count, oldest pending age, overflow count, and degraded count.
- [x] AC-3: Given headless pending output overflows, when the configured overflow policy is `degrade-headless`, then the system degrades the headless terminal, clears bounded pending state, records telemetry, and does not silently drop, overwrite, or join an unbounded pending array.
- [x] AC-4: Given headless output remains healthy, when data is written to the headless terminal, then `routeSessionOutput()` is called only after the headless write commit so `screenSeq` ordering remains consistent for live output, replay, and repair.
- [x] AC-5: Given Wave4 is implemented, then `pendingOutputChunks: string[]`, hot-path `Array.shift()` on pending output, and unbounded pending output `join()` are removed from SessionManager headless output paths.
- [x] AC-6: Given a server WebSocket send is attempted, when the socket is not open, above high water, above hard limit, or returns a send callback error, then a single safe-send policy reports the result, queues or closes only that client as configured, and records bufferedAmount, queued/coalesced bytes, slow-client close count, and send-error telemetry.
- [x] AC-7: Given per-client transport queues are used, then output and control transport queues are separately bounded; output may coalesce adjacent messages by session id within the configured byte/window limits, while control messages are lossless-or-close.
- [x] AC-8: Given replay or screen-repair protocol queues are pending, when WebSocket transport backpressure occurs, then protocol queues remain separate from transport queues and replay/screen-repair ACK, timeout, ordering, and repair-pending behavior continue to pass existing regressions.

| VE-3 | review | independent sub-agent review | AC-1..AC-8 | PH-003 review loop found lifecycle gaps; fixes were applied and final re-review returned `No findings`. |

#### Implementation Notes

- Keep unified `/ws` as the implementation target. Split WebSocket transport is not required for Wave4.
- Do not change shutdown policy, process cleanup mode defaults, UI visuals, or frontend runtime residency behavior in this wave.
- `stabilityModes.headlessQueueMode = observe` preserves the default rollout mode for newly created sessions but still degrades on bounded queue overflow so pending output cannot grow without bound. WebSocket rollback remains possible through `stabilityModes.wsSendMode = direct`; `safe-send-observe` may collect telemetry without enforcement.
- Slow clients must not terminate sessions. They may be closed and recovered by reconnect/replay.

### FR-BGSTAB-011 — Wave 2 verified process-tree termination

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 0.5.5-buildergate-stability |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | buildergate, stability, wave2, session-cleanup, process-tree, termination, feasibility:high |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | stable |
| Verification Method | Server process-tree terminator tests, async SessionManager termination tests, route/workspace lifecycle tests, daemon process-info tests, full server test suite, frontend typecheck, and independent sub-agent review. |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/plug-leak/wave2.md, docs/research/plug-leak/00.index.md, docs/research/2026-06-26.buildergate-wave0-2-evaluation-handoff.md |

#### Requirement

The system shall terminate explicit session, tab, workspace, and restart-owned process trees through an asynchronous verified process-tree termination API that validates process ownership before enforcement, preserves legacy delete compatibility, and reports unverified descendants without broad process-name killing.

#### Rationale

Wave 2 closes the process leak risk identified after Wave 1 observe-only telemetry. Session close paths must wait for safe cleanup decisions, but PID-only or name-based termination can kill unrelated user processes after PID reuse, so enforcement must validate start identity and ownership first.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Given a session has root process metadata with a root PID and best-effort OS start identity, when processCleanup.mode is enforce and an explicit session termination runs, then the system validates the live root process identity before attempting tree termination.
- [x] AC-2: Given root PID, OS start identity, process information, or provider-sampled cwd cannot be verified or mismatches the recorded session metadata, when termination is requested, then no process-tree kill is attempted and cleanup telemetry reports skipped-unverified or degraded state. Windows cwd is not treated as provider-sampled cwd; Windows enforcement relies on verified process start identity and PID-only taskkill.
- [x] AC-3: Given Windows process-tree termination is verified, when enforcement runs, then it invokes taskkill.exe with /PID <verifiedRootPid> /T /F, uses windowsHide, does not use shell: true, and never uses taskkill /IM or process-name broad kill.
- [x] AC-4: Given POSIX process-tree termination is verified, when process group ownership is not verified, then the system uses verified descendant leaf-first termination instead of signaling -rootPid; process-group signaling is allowed only when the process group id is verified as the session-owned root group. If the root exits before a sampled descendant, the surviving sampled descendant remains reported as degraded/unverified instead of falsely completing cleanup.
- [x] AC-5: Given DELETE /api/sessions/:id, deleteTab, deleteWorkspace, and restartTab clean up sessions, when those paths execute, then they route through asynchronous terminateSession or terminateMultipleSessions and await the result while preserving existing HTTP success/failure semantics.
- [x] AC-6: Given terminateMultipleSessions or terminateAllSessions is called, when some session ids are missing or already finalized, then the batch result remains deterministic and reports attempted, terminated, and missing ids without throwing for ordinary missing sessions.
- [x] AC-7: Given Wave2 is implemented, then deleteSession(id) remains as a synchronous compatibility wrapper, shutdown policy changes remain deferred to Wave5, and no raw terminal input/output is added to process cleanup telemetry.

| VE-3 | test | node --test tools/daemon/process-info.test.js tools/daemon/stop-client.test.js | AC-1, AC-2 | Existing daemon process identity tests passed and cover PID reuse/start-time validation helpers. |

Shutdown policy and live marker E2E coverage remain outside this wave unless deterministic local preconditions are added.

### ~~REL-BGSTAB-002 — Wave 5 shutdown cleanup evidence and daemon stop validation~~ [DISCARDED → see REL-BGSTAB-005]

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | reliability |
| Target | 0.5.5-buildergate-stability |
| Status | discarded |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | buildergate, stability, wave5, shutdown, daemon-stop, session-cleanup, soak, feasibility:high |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | Server graceful shutdown tests, daemon stop evidence tests, daemon sentinel regressions, SpecKiwi validation, independent sub-agent review, and explicit server-only soak report or remaining-validation report. |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/plug-leak/wave5.md, docs/research/2026-06-27.buildergate-wave5-shutdown-evidence-soak-plan.md, docs/research/2026-06-28.buildergate-wave5-implementation-report.md, docs/research/plug-leak/00.index.md |

#### Requirement

The system shall include bounded session process cleanup in graceful shutdown and expose shutdown session cleanup evidence through the internal shutdown route and daemon stop flow, while preserving verified-owned cleanup semantics and avoiding broad process-name termination.

#### Rationale

Wave5 converts the Wave0-Wave4 cleanup, lifecycle, bounded output, and backpressure work into observable shutdown and daemon-stop evidence. Current shutdown only snapshots and flushes workspace CWD state; stop-client validates only workspace flush evidence. This requirement records the Wave5 research plan in SRS without creating a new functional FR-BGSTAB-013.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Given graceful shutdown starts with active sessions, when shutdown executes, then the observable sequence is CWD watcher stop, active tab CWD snapshot, first workspace flush, bounded owned-session termination, cleanup evidence collection, final workspace flush, and process exit scheduling.
- [x] AC-2: Given /api/internal/shutdown returns a successful response, then the response includes integer non-negative sessionCleanupAttempted, sessionCleanupCompleted, sessionCleanupDegraded, sessionCleanupSkippedUnverified, and remainingVerifiedDescendants fields in addition to existing workspace flush evidence.
- [x] AC-3: Given stop.bat or stop-client receives internal shutdown evidence, when the evidence schema is missing, malformed, or reports remainingVerifiedDescendants greater than zero, then daemon stop fails gracefully without adding taskkill /IM, killall, whole node.exe termination, or any process-name broad kill fallback.
- [x] AC-4: Given session cleanup reports degraded or skipped-unverified observations but no remaining verified-owned descendants, then default daemon stop records warnings while strict server-soak validation treats those observations as failure.
- [x] AC-5: Given cleanup evidence is computed, then remainingVerifiedDescendants counts only verified-owned remaining descendants and does not infer verified ownership from public telemetry that only reports aggregate remainingDescendants.
- [x] AC-6: Given shutdown cleanup runs, then it preserves FR-BGSTAB-011 ownership verification principles, FR-BGSTAB-010 stopped/non-recoverable lifecycle semantics, and FR-BGSTAB-012 queue/backpressure separation without frontend or split WebSocket changes.
- [x] AC-7: Given live/server-only soak, bash or WSL scenarios, or long-duration browser memory/input-lag validation are not executed or unavailable, then the completion report records them as skipped or remaining operational validation instead of completed coverage.

#### Requirement

The browser Settings page shall let authenticated users inspect and update the selected Wave 6 resource limit fields through the existing Settings snapshot, validation, save, and apply-summary flows while keeping server-only resource sections out of the public runtime configuration projection and keeping reserved fields out of the Settings UI/PATCH contract.

#### Rationale

Wave0 recovered the server-side resource limit contract and Wave4 consumes server output/backpressure limits, but users still need a safe Settings UI path to tune the selected limits without editing config.json5 manually or leaking server-only values to the browser runtime config.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Given the Settings snapshot includes Wave6 resource limit capabilities, when the Settings page renders, then available selected fields are shown in resource limit cards with the existing Field, scope badge, validation banner, and save flow while unavailable or reserved fields remain hidden.
- [x] AC-2: Given a user changes one or more selected resource limit fields, when the Settings page builds a PATCH request, then it sends only changed leaf values as a nested resourceLimits partial and does not include unchanged, unavailable, telemetry, or stabilityModes fields.
- [x] AC-3: Given a local numeric resource field value is empty, non-finite, non-integer, below min, above max, or violates step constraints from FieldCapability, when the user attempts to save, then the existing validation path prevents save or surfaces the error without silently clamping the value.
- [x] AC-4: Given the server rejects a syntactically valid but cross-field-invalid resource limit patch, when the Settings save request fails, then the existing error banner shows an observable error and no client-side fallback pretends the change succeeded.
- [x] AC-5: Given a valid selected resource limit patch is saved, then config.json5 remains the persistence source of truth, the response reports truthful changedKeys and applySummary scopes, and browser-needed public resource values are visible through /api/runtime-config after save or reload while server-only headless/ws/telemetry resource sections are not public.
- [x] AC-6: Given Wave6 is complete, then no process cleanup policy, terminal runtime residency behavior, visible output scheduler behavior, browser WebSocket backpressure behavior, split WebSocket behavior, or broad process termination behavior is changed by this wave.

### FR-BGSTAB-014 — Wave 7 frontend runtime resource consumers

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 0.5.5-buildergate-stability |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | buildergate, stability, wave7, frontend-runtime, resource-limits, feasibility:high |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | stable |
| Verification Method | Focused frontend runtime-config, residency, snapshot, hidden output, visible output recovery, WebSocket backpressure, typecheck/build, E2E/manual validation, and independent sub-agent review. |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/2026-06-29.buildergate-wave7-frontend-runtime-implementation-plan.md, docs/research/plug-leak/wave7.md, docs/research/plug-leak/wave6.md, docs/research/plug-leak/00.index.md |

#### Requirement

The browser frontend shall consume the Wave6 runtime resource-limit configuration in the default product path so terminal runtime residency, hidden terminal output, snapshot storage, workspace layout cleanup, visible output overflow recovery, and browser input WebSocket send failure handling remain bounded and observable during long-running BuilderGate sessions.

#### Rationale

Wave6 made selected resource limits editable through Settings but deliberately did not change runtime behavior. Wave7 completes the intended stability path by wiring those limits into frontend runtime consumers and by making hidden or overflow recovery failures explicit instead of silently accumulating browser work.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Given Settings saves a selected browser resource limit, when the save succeeds, then the frontend runtime config is reloaded and subscribers observe a runtimeConfigVersion change without requiring a full page reload.
- [x] AC-2: Given frontendRuntimeResidency is the default product path, when terminal runtime residency is computed, then mounted terminal runtimes are bounded by workspaceRuntime limits while visible terminals remain pinned and evicted tabs do not remain in TerminalRuntimeLayer or terminalRefsMap.
- [x] AC-3: Given snapshot resource limits are loaded, when terminal snapshots are parsed, saved, evicted, or tombstones are checked, then per-snapshot size, total storage budget, maxEntries, and tombstoneTtlMs are enforced using runtime config values.
- [x] AC-4: Given a workspace or tab is deleted, when mosaic layout and terminal snapshot cleanup runs, then stale localStorage layout/snapshot state is removed and delete-triggered unmount cleanup does not recreate it.
- [x] AC-5: Given hidden output uses the default Wave7 policy, when terminal output arrives while hidden, then xterm writes are skipped and visible restoration clears dirty state only after snapshot restore, repair, or equivalent authoritative recovery succeeds.
- [x] AC-6: Given visible output exceeds scheduler limits, when overflow occurs, then the terminal enters an observable recovery state and requests an authoritative repair without creating duplicate repair storms or silently treating failed recovery as success.
- [x] AC-7: Given browser WebSocket input send is backpressured or socket.send throws, when TerminalContainer sends input, then WebSocketContext returns an explicit failure reason, ws_input_sent is emitted only for real send success, and input content remains out of debug logs.
- [x] AC-8: Given Wave7 is complete, then write-hidden and legacy runtime residency remain explicit rollback/fallback settings only; the normal default product path is bounded and snapshot-restore based.

#### Research / Analysis

- docs/research/2026-06-29.buildergate-wave7-frontend-runtime-implementation-plan.md

#### Implementation Notes

- Wave7 default runtime path is `frontendRuntimeResidency: "bounded"` and `hiddenOutputPolicy: "snapshot-restore"`; legacy/write-hidden remain explicit rollback settings.
- Visible output overflow now creates a `visible-output-recovery` input barrier until authoritative screen snapshot/repair success clears it; the barrier defers input outbox flushing but does not block the repair request required to recover.
- Direct and WebSocket tab deletion prune persisted mosaic layouts for inactive workspaces as well as mounted workspaces.
- Direct and WebSocket workspace deletion update persisted `active_workspace_id` to the next remaining workspace or remove it when no workspaces remain.

### FR-BGSTAB-015 — ConPTY transport and recovery Settings contract

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 0.5.5-buildergate-stability |
| Status | in_progress |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | buildergate, stability, conpty, settings, resource-limits, runtime-config, feasibility:high |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | Settings capability tests, SettingsService patch validation tests, RuntimeConfigStore runtime apply tests, Settings UI unit/E2E tests, and SpecKiwi validation. |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/2026-06-30.buildergate-conpty-output-recovery-input-lag-implementation-plan.md, docs/research/2026-06-30.buildergate-conpty-output-recovery-input-lag-research.ko.md, docs/research/2026-06-28.buildergate-wave6-settings-resource-limits-plan.md, docs/research/2026-06-29.buildergate-wave6-settings-resource-limits-implementation-report.md |

#### Requirement

The Settings and runtime configuration contract shall expose the ConPTY transport and recovery tuning values that have runtime consumers, including safe-send mode selection, output coalescing interval, per-client control/output queue limits, server buffered high/hard limits, transport retry interval, replay input/tail limits, screen repair pending-output limit, visible/recovery write-gate budgets, scrollback and repair-delay limits, and telemetry thresholds, while preserving validation, persistence, runtime apply summaries, and explicit unavailability for fields without runtime consumers.

#### Rationale

The ConPTY implementation plan requires every new queue size, flush interval, timeout, threshold, and mode value to be configurable from the Settings page instead of being hard-coded. Wave6 intentionally exposed only selected resource fields; this requirement records the later ConPTY-specific Settings expansion without discarding the Wave6 completion record.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Given a ConPTY transport or recovery runtime variable is listed in the ConPTY Settings matrix and has an active runtime consumer, when Settings capabilities are loaded, then the variable is exposed as editable with constraints and applyScope instead of remaining hidden or explicitly unavailable.
- [ ] AC-2: Given a user saves ConPTY transport or recovery tuning values, when the Settings PATCH is valid, then config persistence remains the source of truth, changedKeys and applySummary identify the changed leaf keys, and immediate-scope values are applied to RuntimeConfigStore, WsRouter, SessionManager, or frontend runtime consumers as applicable.
- [ ] AC-3: Given a ConPTY transport or recovery Settings value is empty, non-finite, out of range, step-invalid, or violates a cross-field relationship, when save is attempted, then the client or server validation path rejects the value without silently clamping it.
- [ ] AC-4: Given wsSendMode remains direct, when outputCoalesceWindowMs is displayed or saved, then the UI or capability hint makes the safe-send dependency observable so the value is not presented as effective in direct mode.
- [ ] AC-5: Given a setting has no runtime consumer or only one literal behavior, when Settings renders, then it remains unavailable or read-only instead of being exposed as an editable control.
- [ ] AC-6: Given the ConPTY Settings matrix is implemented, then it includes wsSendMode, outputCoalesceWindowMs, per-client control/output queues, server buffered high/hard limits, transportFlushRetryMs, replay tail/input TTL limits, screenRepairQueuedOutputMaxBytes, recoveryReadyReleaseTimeoutMs, recoveryWriteLatencyWarnMs, visible/recovery output budgets, scrollbackLines, repair delays, visible recovery retry count, recentEventLimit, input latency thresholds, and recovery-ready thresholds with the planned immediate or new-session apply scopes.

#### Implementation Notes

- Keep wsSendMode default as direct until evidence supports a rollout. Treat stabilityModes.wsSendMode as the ConPTY rollout switch; do not generally expose Wave6/Wave7 stability modes that remain out of scope. Do not silently switch Codex or ConPTY sessions to winpty, pipe, or another PTY backend as a workaround.

| Verification Method | Server safe-send unit tests, synthetic redraw flood queue tests, recovery ordering regression tests, and observability snapshot assertions. |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/2026-06-30.buildergate-conpty-output-recovery-input-lag-implementation-plan.md, docs/research/2026-06-30.buildergate-conpty-output-recovery-input-lag-research.ko.md, docs/research/plug-leak/wave4.md, docs/research/2026-06-17.buildergate-backend-backpressure-cleanup-plan.md |

#### Requirement

The server WebSocket safe-send path shall use priority-aware transport queues and projected buffered-byte decisions so unrelated control traffic is not starved by terminal output flood, while server-sent terminal recovery payload ordering for snapshot, repair, replay tail, and session readiness remains preserved.

#### Rationale

ConPTY and AI TUI sessions can emit high-frequency redraw output. Wave4 bounded transport bytes, but this requirement adds priority-aware draining, projected-byte enforcement, and recovery transaction barriers without changing the default direct rollout.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Given wsSendMode is safe-send-observe or safe-send-enforce and terminal output is queued, when an unrelated control message is enqueued, then the transport drain can send eligible control traffic before unrelated terminal output backlog without reordering an active recovery transaction.
- [ ] AC-2: Given a server-sent terminal recovery transaction contains snapshot, repair, replay tail, or session readiness messages, when priority drain runs, then those transaction messages preserve their required terminal ordering; queued input release is verified by FR-BGSTAB-017.
- [ ] AC-3: Given a WebSocket message would make bufferedAmount plus payload bytes exceed configured high-water or hard-limit thresholds, when send is attempted, then the safe-send policy observes, queues, or closes only that client according to mode instead of directly sending a giant payload.
- [ ] AC-4: Given output coalescing is enabled, when adjacent output messages belong to the same session and remain within configured byte limits, then they may be coalesced; snapshot, repair, status, readiness, input rejection, and cross-session messages are not coalesced together.
- [ ] AC-5: Given a slow client triggers safe-send enforcement, then the session is not killed solely because that client is slow and transport queue telemetry records the decision.

#### Research / Analysis

- Implementation plan phase 2 requires control/output lane separation, projectedBufferedBytes checks, same-session-only coalescing, and recovery transaction barriers.

#### Implementation Notes

- Keep wsSendMode default direct until baseline and observe/enforce evidence support a default rollout. Do not make split WebSocket a prerequisite for this requirement.

### REL-BGSTAB-003 — Byte-aware replay tail and screen repair overflow recovery

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | reliability |
| Target | 0.5.5-buildergate-stability |
| Status | planned |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | buildergate, stability, conpty, replay, screen-repair, reliability, feasibility:high |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | Server replay/repair regression tests, UTF-8 and ANSI boundary tests, overflow-to-refresh tests, and telemetry redaction assertions. |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/2026-06-30.buildergate-conpty-output-recovery-input-lag-implementation-plan.md, docs/research/2026-06-30.buildergate-conpty-output-recovery-input-lag-research.ko.md, docs/research/plug-leak/wave4.md |

#### Requirement

Replay tail and screen repair pending output shall be bounded by byte-aware queues that preserve terminal payload boundaries and convert overflow into explicit snapshot refresh or recovery flow instead of flushing oversized concatenated output directly to clients.

#### Rationale

The ConPTY plan identifies recovery tail and screen repair overflow as paths that can turn bounded output work back into giant writes. Current replay state is string-tail based and repair overflow can concatenate pending output with new data. This requirement makes recovery pressure bounded, observable, and restartable.
- [2026-08-18] String-tail 전제는 IR-BGSTAB-001 의 바이너리 데이터 평면에서 encoded-byte tail 로 대체된다. AC-1 의 UTF-8 경계 비분할 요구는 프레임 내부 터미널 페이로드에 그대로 적용된다.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Given replay output is pending behind a screen snapshot ACK, when output exceeds the configured replay tail budget, then truncation is byte-aware, records truncation metadata, and does not split UTF-8 characters.
- [ ] AC-2: Given replay or repair pending output contains terminal control sequences, when chunking or truncation occurs, then payload boundary handling is covered by deterministic tests that prove no invalid text or ordering corruption is introduced.
- [ ] AC-3: Given screen repair pending output would exceed configured byte or chunk limits, when the overflow is detected, then the repair transaction is rejected or aborted with an observable reason and a snapshot refresh or equivalent recovery path is requested instead of sending one oversized concatenated output payload.
- [ ] AC-4: Given recovery timeout or overflow occurs, then telemetry or debug events identify the reason without storing raw terminal input or raw terminal output content in durable artifacts.
- [ ] AC-5: Given replay and repair limits are introduced, then those limits are sourced from Settings-backed runtime configuration rather than hard-coded constants.

- Implementation plan phase 3 requires replay tail byte rings, screen repair overflow abort/refresh, and explicit recovery events instead of giant output flushes.

#### Implementation Notes

- Preserve existing snapshot and repair token semantics. Do not use repair overflow as a direct output fast path.

### FR-BGSTAB-017 — Frontend recovery write gate and queued input release barrier

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 0.5.5-buildergate-stability |
| Status | planned |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | buildergate, stability, conpty, frontend-runtime, xterm, recovery, input, feasibility:high |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | Frontend recovery scheduler unit tests, TerminalView recovery contract tests, TerminalContainer recovery ordering tests, WebSocket classification tests, and Playwright redraw/recovery checks at https://localhost:2222. |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/2026-06-30.buildergate-conpty-output-recovery-input-lag-implementation-plan.md, docs/research/2026-06-30.buildergate-conpty-output-recovery-input-lag-research.ko.md, docs/research/2026-06-29.buildergate-wave7-frontend-runtime-implementation-plan.md, docs/research/plug-leak/wave7.md |

#### Requirement

The frontend terminal runtime shall apply snapshots, screen repairs, and ACK-pending recovery tail output through a bounded recovery write gate instead of large direct xterm writes, and shall release queued input only after recovery writes drain or an observable configured timeout is reached.

#### Rationale

Live visible output already uses a scheduler, but the ConPTY plan found that snapshot restore, snapshot replacement, and screen repair can still write large payloads directly to xterm. That can block the browser main thread and make input appear unavailable during heavy AI TUI redraws.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Given local snapshot restore, authoritative snapshot replacement, fallback snapshot replacement, or screen repair applies terminal content, when the frontend writes that content to xterm, then the write goes through a bounded recovery scheduler rather than a large direct term.write call.
- [ ] AC-2: Given the client has acknowledged a snapshot and receives output before session readiness, when that output belongs to the recovery tail, then it is routed through the recovery write gate rather than the live visible output lane.
- [ ] AC-3: Given session readiness arrives while the recovery write gate still has pending work, then queued input is not released to the PTY until the recovery gate drains or a configured recovery release timeout emits an observable release event.
- [ ] AC-4: Given recovery write gate overflow occurs, then the terminal is marked stale or recovery-needed and a server refresh or repair path is requested without treating the failed write as success.
- [ ] AC-5: Given recovery scheduling is reset or superseded by a new snapshot or repair token, then stale callbacks from canceled writes cannot mutate the current terminal state.
- [ ] AC-6: Given a snapshot is applied before replay tail recovery, then the client sends screen-snapshot:ready only after the snapshot recovery write completes for the current token; timeout, overflow, or stale-token failure emits an observable recovery-needed path instead of a success ACK.

| Code | frontend/src/components/Terminal/TerminalView.tsx:1148-1214 | addition_site | trace_intent=addition_site; add recoverySchedulerRef and recovery write API alongside live output scheduler |
| Code | frontend/src/components/Terminal/TerminalView.tsx:1260-1465 | addition_site | trace_intent=addition_site; route snapshot restore/replacement and screen repair through recovery gate |
| Code | frontend/src/components/Terminal/TerminalContainer.tsx:1296-1688 | addition_site | trace_intent=addition_site; route ACK-tail and gate input release on recovery drain or timeout |
| Code | frontend/src/contexts/WebSocketContext.tsx:141-322 | addition_site | trace_intent=addition_site; preserve reconnect/grace ordering and classify recovery messages |

#### Research / Analysis

- Implementation plan phase 4 requires a separate recovery write gate, ACK-tail routing, recovery drain wait, timeout-based release event, and stale repair token protection.

#### Implementation Notes

- Reuse terminalOutputScheduler principles where possible. Do not restore the legacy direct write path as rollback; degraded rollback should lower recovery budgets and force server snapshot refresh on overflow.

| Related Docs | docs/research/2026-06-30.buildergate-conpty-output-recovery-input-lag-implementation-plan.md, docs/research/2026-06-30.buildergate-conpty-output-recovery-input-lag-research.ko.md, docs/research/2026-06-24.buildergate-slowdown-session-process-leak-followup.md, docs/research/2026-06-27.buildergate-wave5-shutdown-evidence-soak-plan.md |

#### Requirement

The ConPTY recovery work shall provide telemetry, debug-capture correlation, deterministic redraw-flood tests, and soak evidence that measure queue pressure, recovery ordering, input latency, and cleanup results without persisting raw terminal input or output payloads.

#### Rationale

The plan states that the exact human perception of ConPTY slowness is difficult to reproduce reliably, so completion must be judged through measurable bottlenecks: queue depth, projected buffered amount, control flush latency, recovery-to-ready latency, input write correlation, and bounded soak cleanup evidence.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Given baseline collection runs before behavior rollout, when wsSendMode is direct, safe-send-observe, or safe-send-enforce, then the run records the Settings snapshot, input and recovery timing, transportOutputQueuedBytes, transportControlQueuedBytes, headless pending bytes, coalesce count, queue depths, projected buffer maximum, control flush latency, and recovery-to-ready latency.
- [ ] AC-2: Given telemetry or debug capture records input and recovery timing, then correlation identifiers and timestamps are recorded without durable storage of raw terminal input, raw terminal output, snapshot content, or ANSI patch content.
- [ ] AC-3: Given output flood is tested, when the deterministic PowerShell ANSI redraw workload runs, then the test asserts queue limits, control starvation prevention, recovery ordering, and input release behavior rather than requiring an exact human-perceived slowness reproduction.
- [ ] AC-4: Given validation is run by an agent, then Playwright or manual browser validation targets https://localhost:2222, health checks use http://localhost:2221/health, server startup uses start.bat --port 2222 when needed, shutdown uses stop.bat, and no broad node.exe termination is used.
- [ ] AC-5: Given final validation completes, then deterministic ConPTY redraw-flood E2E at https://localhost:2222 and soak pass evidence require output loss/reorder/duplication=0, input_write_failed=0, unexplained server_input_rejected=0, transportOutputQueuedBytes, transportControlQueuedBytes, and headless pending bytes returned to baseline, and remainingVerifiedDescendants=0; unsupported bash, WSL, or long-duration browser memory coverage is reported as remaining operational validation.

### FR-BGSTAB-018 — Empty fallback snapshot convergence and placeholder suppression

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | functional |
| Target | 0.5.5-buildergate-stability |
| Status | planned |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | buildergate, stability, frontend-runtime, terminal, fallback-snapshot, recovery, xterm, feasibility:high |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | Frontend fallback recovery unit tests, TerminalContainer recovery contract tests, fake-terminal ACK tests, WebSocket replay ordering tests, and Playwright/debug-event validation at https://localhost:2222 when practical. |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/2026-07-01.buildergate-empty-fallback-snapshot-placeholder-research.md, docs/research/2026-06-30.buildergate-conpty-output-recovery-input-lag-implementation-plan.md, docs/research/2026-06-30.buildergate-conpty-output-recovery-input-lag-research.ko.md |

#### Requirement

The frontend terminal runtime shall treat empty fallback screen snapshots as a non-destructive recovery state that converges without repeatedly writing shell-looking placeholder text into xterm, while preserving replay ACK ordering and recovery-pending semantics.

#### Rationale

The current empty fallback path writes `[BuilderGate] Fallback snapshot unavailable. Waiting for new output...` into the terminal buffer whenever local restore fails. Repeated degraded or truncated-empty fallback snapshots can therefore pollute scrollback and reset terminal content without actually recovering authoritative output.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Given a screen-snapshot has mode=fallback and empty data, when a valid local viewport snapshot exists for the same session and geometry, then the frontend restores that local snapshot, acknowledges the replay token, and does not render the fallback-unavailable placeholder.
- [ ] AC-2: Given a screen-snapshot has mode=fallback and empty data and local restore fails, when the same recovery generation receives repeated empty fallback snapshots, then the frontend does not repeatedly call snapshot replacement with the fallback-unavailable placeholder and preserves the current terminal buffer unless a one-time compatibility placeholder is explicitly still required.
- [ ] AC-3: Given empty fallback handling runs without valid snapshot content, when the replay token is current, then the client still sends screen-snapshot:ready or an explicit observable send-failure event so server replay output is not left blocked by placeholder rendering.
- [ ] AC-4: Given empty fallback placeholder or no-op handling occurs while hidden-output or visible-output recovery is pending, then it does not clear hidden-output recovery, finish visible-output recovery, release queued input as successful recovery, or mark terminal recovery successful solely because placeholder/no-op handling ran.
- [ ] AC-5: Given real output, an authoritative snapshot, a non-empty fallback snapshot, successful local restore, successful screen repair, or session generation change occurs, then empty-fallback suppression state is reset so later recovery generations can be handled independently.
- [ ] AC-6: Given the empty fallback convergence behavior is implemented, then deterministic frontend unit tests cover repeated empty fallback suppression, ACK preservation, recovery-pending preservation, suppression reset conditions, and no raw terminal payload persistence.

#### Research / Analysis

- Five-subagent investigation on 2026-07-01 found that the placeholder is frontend-generated at TerminalContainer.tsx:131 and written through the empty fallback path at TerminalContainer.tsx:1419-1433 after local restore failure.

#### Implementation Notes

- Prefer not writing empty fallback placeholder text into xterm at all. If compatibility requires a visible message, bound it to at most one write per recovery generation and keep it out of successful recovery accounting.

### REL-BGSTAB-004 — Fallback replay output preservation and degraded snapshot convergence

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | reliability |
| Target | 0.5.5-buildergate-stability |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | buildergate, stability, websocket, replay, fallback-snapshot, headless, output-preservation, feasibility:high |
| Risk | high |
| Stability | stable |
| Verification Method | Server WsRouter replay tests, SessionManager degraded snapshot tests, bounded replay-tail tests, safe-send ordering tests, and telemetry/redaction assertions. |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/2026-07-01.buildergate-empty-fallback-snapshot-placeholder-research.md, docs/research/2026-06-30.buildergate-conpty-output-recovery-input-lag-implementation-plan.md, docs/research/2026-06-17.buildergate-backend-backpressure-cleanup-plan.md |

#### Requirement

The server replay and degraded snapshot paths shall preserve ordered terminal output across empty fallback snapshots, replay refreshes, and replay ACK timeouts, and shall converge repeated degraded fallback states without silently dropping queued output or indefinitely churning replay tokens.

#### Rationale

The investigation found that output queued behind a screen-snapshot ACK is flushed on ACK, but the reviewed replay timeout path deletes pending replay state and flushes queued input without flushing queued output. Empty fallback placeholder handling can delay ACK, so the timeout path must preserve output rather than converting a visual recovery issue into output loss.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [x] AC-1: Given terminal output is queued while a fallback screen snapshot is awaiting ACK, when a matching screen-snapshot:ready is received, then queued output is flushed in order before or with session readiness as today.
- [x] AC-2: Given terminal output is queued while a fallback screen snapshot is awaiting ACK, when replay ACK timeout or refresh-timeout occurs, then queued output is flushed, moved to a bounded replay tail, or recovered through an explicit fresh snapshot/repair transaction instead of being silently dropped.
- [x] AC-3: Given refreshReplaySnapshots encounters an unchanged empty fallback snapshot for the same client/session while replay is still pending, then it does not indefinitely rotate replay tokens or resend equivalent empty fallback snapshots faster than the configured/debounced recovery cadence.
- [x] AC-4: Given headless terminal degrades after create, write, resize, serialize, or queue overflow, then the degraded recovery path preserves ordered cached, unsnapshotted, and pending output in a bounded replay buffer without duplication, and exposes whether fallback snapshot data is empty because no recoverable data exists or because data was withheld.
- [x] AC-5: Given a degraded replay buffer contains recoverable bounded content, then the server either sends safe fallback data, starts an explicit snapshot/repair recovery transaction, or records an observable reason for withholding the data; it must not leave callers with an opaque empty fallback loop.
- [x] AC-6: Given fallback replay preservation is implemented, then server regression tests cover ACK success, ACK timeout, refresh timeout, repeated empty fallback refresh suppression, degraded buffer preservation without duplication, UTF-8-safe bounded output, and no session kill caused solely by a slow client.

#### Implementation Notes

### OBS-BGSTAB-002 — Empty fallback snapshot diagnostics and regression evidence

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | observability |
| Target | 0.5.5-buildergate-stability |
| Status | planned |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | buildergate, stability, observability, fallback-snapshot, diagnostics, telemetry, regression, feasibility:high |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | Telemetry shape tests, debug redaction tests, frontend fallback regression tests, server replay/degraded regression tests, SpecKiwi validation, and independent sub-agent review. |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/2026-07-01.buildergate-empty-fallback-snapshot-placeholder-research.md, docs/research/2026-06-30.buildergate-conpty-output-recovery-input-lag-implementation-plan.md, docs/research/2026-06-30.buildergate-conpty-output-recovery-input-lag-research.ko.md, docs/research/2026-06-27.buildergate-wave5-shutdown-evidence-soak-plan.md |

#### Requirement

The BuilderGate terminal recovery diagnostics shall expose bounded, redacted evidence for empty fallback snapshot loops, placeholder suppression, degraded headless phases, replay timeout output preservation, and recovery convergence so repeated fallback noise can be diagnosed without storing raw terminal payloads.

#### Rationale

The user-visible placeholder loop is caused by interactions between frontend empty fallback handling, server degraded snapshots, and replay/ACK state. Operators need phase/reason counters and regression evidence to distinguish frontend suppression, server degraded headless, oversized snapshots, replay refresh churn, and timeout recovery without inspecting raw terminal output.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: Given the frontend receives an empty fallback snapshot, then debug or telemetry events distinguish local restore success, placeholder/no-op applied, placeholder suppressed, ACK sent, and ACK send failure without durable raw snapshot or terminal payload storage.
- [ ] AC-2: Given the server sends fallback snapshots, then observability distinguishes degraded headless, truncated-empty oversized snapshot, no recoverable data, recoverable data withheld, replay refresh, and repair replay origins.
- [ ] AC-3: Given headless terminal degrades, then diagnostics record phase counts for create, write, resize, serialize, and queue overflow, plus bounded degraded replay buffer byte metrics and oldest pending write age.
- [ ] AC-4: Given replay ACK timeout or refresh-timeout occurs, then diagnostics record queued output bytes, selected preservation action, queued input counts, and whether a fresh snapshot/repair transaction was requested.
- [ ] AC-5: Given repeated empty fallback snapshots are suppressed, debounced, or escalated, then counters expose suppression count, repeated token churn count, last fallback seq, and convergence reason.
- [ ] AC-6: Given final validation is performed, then research or implementation evidence includes deterministic unit/server tests for fallback suppression, replay timeout output preservation, degraded buffer preservation, redaction, and SpecKiwi validation with no raw terminal input/output persisted.

### PERF-BGSTAB-001 — Frame time-budgeted visible output flush with input yield

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Type | performance |
| Target | 0.5.5-buildergate-stability |
| Status | implemented |
| Priority | high |
| Tags | buildergate, stability, xterm, output, performance, feasibility:high |
| Risk | medium |
| Stability | evolving |
| Verification Method | Terminal output scheduler unit tests (frontend/tests/unit/terminalOutputScheduler.test.ts) asserting per-frame multi-chunk drain, input-yield, code-point safety, and the default per-frame time-budget range, plus server config schema/default verification, with a failing regression test authored before implementation (TDD). |
| GitHub Issue | - |
| Related Docs | docs/research/2026-07-02.buildergate-native-performance-54-sessions-deep-analysis.md |

#### Requirement

The visible terminal output scheduler shall flush queued output within a configurable per-frame time budget by draining multiple output chunks per animation frame instead of a single chunk per frame, shall yield the current frame when pending keyboard input is detected so input processing is not starved, and shall restore the operational visibleFlushBudgetBytes override in server/config.json5 to the schema-default byte budget so visible output is not throttled below the intended runtime limit.

#### Rationale

A single-chunk-per-animation-frame flush cadence and an under-provisioned operational visibleFlushBudgetBytes override (16384 versus the 262144 schema default) throttle visible terminal output far below the intended runtime limit, which is a primary contributor to sluggish native performance at 54-session scale. Draining multiple chunks within a bounded per-frame time budget while still yielding for pending input restores throughput without reintroducing the main-thread monopolization the bounded scheduler was designed to prevent.

#### Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-1: AC-1: Given more visible output is queued than one chunk can drain within the configured per-frame time budget, when a frame flush runs, then the scheduler drains multiple chunks within that frame until the time budget is exhausted or the queue is empty, and it never splits a multibyte code point (preserving FR-BGSTAB-003 AC-2).
- [ ] AC-2: AC-2: Given pending keyboard input is detected while the scheduler is draining within a frame, when the scheduler evaluates its input-yield check, then it stops draining for that frame and yields so queued input processing is not starved.
- [ ] AC-3: AC-3: Given no explicit per-frame time budget is configured, when the scheduler initializes, then the default per-frame flush time budget is within the 6-8ms range.
- [ ] AC-4: AC-4: Given server/config.json5 previously set resourceLimits.terminal.visibleFlushBudgetBytes to 16384, when the operational configuration is loaded after this change, then the effective visible flush byte budget equals the schema default 262144 and no operational override throttles it below that value.
- [ ] AC-5: AC-5: Given this change modifies scheduler drain cadence, when it is implemented, then an automated regression test asserting per-frame multi-chunk drain, input-yield behavior, and no code-point splitting is added to frontend/tests/unit/terminalOutputScheduler.test.ts, fails before implementation (red), and passes after implementation (green).

#### Verification Evidence

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
